PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 30, 1899.
210 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-sEP., NOTICES-, TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 342. FOOCHOW DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN CHARACTER OF TURNABOUT LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the character of the light at Turnabout has been changed. The new light is dioptric, occulting, of the first order, showing a fixed white light varied by an eclipse every I5 seconds. It is elevated 257 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather should be visible for a distance of 23 nautical miles. SHANGHAI, 18t Aly 1899. No. 343. SANTU DISTRICT. SAMSAH BAY. KAISER ROCK Buoy. NOTICE is hereby given that the lately discovered Kaiser Rock, situated to the westward of Crag Island, Samsah Bay, has been marked by a black conical buoy. The buoy is moored in 17 fathoms of water at low water of spring tides, and lies about I &quot;cable N.N.W. from the rock. Inward-bound vessels must pass this buoy on their port hand. From the rock the eastern end of Santu bears N. 220 E., and the northern end of Cone Island bears S. 540 E. At low water of spring tides the depth of water over the rock is about 4 feet. The bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 8th jiy 1899.
1899.] NOTIFICATIONS. 211 No. SK. FM0CHOW DISTRICT. IAITAN STRAIT. Pms Roo BwACON. NormE is hereby given that an iron tripod beacon, surmounted by a spherical cage 6 feet in diameter, has been placed on Pass Rock, near its northern extremity. The total height of the structure is 26 feet, and at least I2 feet of it will reMain risible at high water of highest spring tides. The beacon with its cage is painted bkwlck. SHAKL*HAI, 19th JU1Y I&quot;~. No. 3W. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN NATURE OF THE &quot;NEWCHWANG&quot; LIGHT-VESSEL. NoTcE is hereby given that the temporary light-vessel Omega has been withdrawn and replaced by a light-vessel having the following characteristics:- The light is catoptric, showing a white flash at intervals of j minute. It is elevated 35 feet above the sea, and in clear weather should be visible for a distance of ii nautical miles. A small white light will be exhibited from the forestay, at a height of 6 feet above the rail, for the purpose of showing in what direction the vessel is riding. The light-vessel is painted red, with the word &quot;NEwcHWANG&quot; in white letters on each side, and she has three masts, the main-mast being surmounted by a black ball. During thick or foggy weather a steam fog horn will be sounded at intervals of io seconds. If the light-vessel be driven from her proper position to one where she is of no use as a guide to shipping, the usual light will not be shown, but a fixed red light will be ex- hibited at each end of the vessel; the ball will be struck as soon as possible, and till struck it will be surmounted by a red flag. SHANGHAI, 18t Setember 1899.
212 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [TULY-8EPT., 1899.] No. 346. FOOCHOW DISTRICT. ENTRANCE TO MIN RIVER. PEAK SHOAL Buoy. NOTICE is hereby given that the bank to the southward of the south point of Sharp Peak Island, the existence of which was notified in Notice to Mariners No. 34o, has now been marked by a red conical buoy. The buoy is moored on the southern edge of the bank, in i i feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy the southern extremity of Sharp Peak Island bears N. 40 W., magnetic, distant 31 cables. Inward-bound vessels should pass this buoy on their starboard hand. SmN AI, iet September 1899.

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1899.
230 OUSTOMS GAZETTE. ULY-SEPT., (Received IA4th October 1899.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 7TR OCTOBER 1899. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1899, July-September. b. The Custom House (I f ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Ek.7 30 and Hk.Ta 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (C * ) Changehow (* g), and Fotochow ( ) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.-The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:- i0. CAPSUIMOON. JUNKS. JULY. AUGusT. SErTENBER. 240 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. JLY- (.Received i8th October 1899.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, I ITH1 OCTOBER 1899. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1899, July-September. b. The Custom House ( 4 ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Bk.Ta 3o and Hk.?&#39; 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the H1oppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowehow (, ) and Chienshan (&#39; (i) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (6 ), Kuttai (&quot; L)f and Kwanchiap (E ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
